{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-5-5","id":"msg_011CgDgQZB5ydBvttQ6Z6xxx","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BEGIN ARTIFACT ANALYSIS.md ===\n# Blueprint Analysis: LayersTesting\n\n## Commander Expectations\n\n- assert the product is an executable `ledger` that keeps cash balances for a small shop in a relational database\n- assert every command either completes in full or changes nothing\n- assert every report is computed from the database and matches the known answers\n- assert the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` exits zero\n- assert no account balance goes negative after any command\n- assert all persistence goes through the shipped persistence library, selected by `DATABASE_URL`\n\n## Crew\n\n| Crew | Charge |\n|---|---|\n| Commander | Defines intent and decides what done means. |\n| Team Lead | Confirms epic completeness and stakeholder expectations. |\n| Planning Crew | Authors atomic specifications and the ordered Manifest. |\n| Shipyard Crew | Builds the tickets without synchronous Commander access. |\n\n## Story List\n\n### Feature: Schema and Persistence\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| PERSIST-001 | Resolve the database target | `DATABASE_URL`, then `DATABASE_PATH`, then `data/app.db`, selects the database; a `postgres://` or `postgresql://` value is a PostgreSQL DSN; other values are SQLite paths with an optional `sqlite://` prefix. |\n| PERSIST-002 | Define the three-table schema | `accounts`, `categories`, and `transactions` exist with the keys and references in the Database section; amounts are integer cents. |\n| PERSIST-003 | Initialize an empty schema | `ledger init` drops the three tables if present, creates them empty, and writes nothing. |\n| PERSIST-004 | Count table rows | `ledger count` writes `table,rows` then the `accounts`, `categories`, and `transactions` rows. |\n\n### Feature: Input Validation\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| VALID-001 | Validate codes, names, descriptions, and memos | Codes are 1 to 12 characters from `A-Z` and `0-9`; names and descriptions are 1 to 40 characters; memos are 0 to 60 characters; none contains a comma or newline; violations exit 3. |\n| VALID-002 | Validate and format amounts | Input matches `-?DIGITS[.D[D]]`, is never zero, and has at most two decimals; output has exactly two decimals and a leading `-` when negative. |\n| VALID-003 | Validate calendar dates | Dates are real calendar dates in `YYYY-MM-DD`; `2026-02-30` exits 3. |\n\n### Feature: Command Line Interface\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| CLI-001 | Dispatch commands and reject usage errors | Unknown command, table, or report, or a wrong argument count, exits 2 with one stderr line; every command has help text. |\n| CLI-002 | Write CSV success output and failure diagnostics | Success writes header and rows as CSV with no quoting, no trailing spaces, and `\\n` endings; `init` writes nothing; failures write nothing to stdout. |\n| CLI-003 | Provide the `ledger` executable at the application root | An executable named `ledger` at the application root runs each command when invoked by the harness from that root. |\n\n### Feature: Bulk Load\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LOAD-001 | Load accounts from a CSV file | A file whose header matches `account,name` loads all rows or none; the seed file loads 4 rows; writes `table,loaded` and `accounts,<rows>`. |\n| LOAD-002 | Load categories from a CSV file | A file whose header matches `category,description` loads all rows or none; the seed file loads 7 rows. |\n| LOAD-003 | Load transactions from a CSV file | A file whose header matches the transaction columns keeps each `txn_id`; any rejected row rejects the file; the seed file loads 120 rows; an unreadable file exits 1. |\n\n### Feature: Accounts\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| ACCT-001 | Add an account | `account add` writes the new account row; a duplicate code or invalid field exits 3. |\n| ACCT-002 | Get an account | `account get` writes the account row with its transaction count and balance; an absent code exits 4. |\n| ACCT-003 | Rename an account | `account rename` writes the updated row; an absent code exits 4; an invalid name exits 3. |\n| ACCT-004 | Delete an account | `account delete` writes `deleted` and the code; an account with transactions is refused with exit 3; an absent code exits 4. |\n\n### Feature: Transactions\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| TXN-001 | Post a transaction | `txn post` writes the new row with id one more than the largest present, or 1 in an empty table; an unknown account or category exits 3. |\n| TXN-002 | Get a transaction | `txn get` writes the row; an absent id exits 4. |\n| TXN-003 | Amend a transaction amount | `txn amend` changes only the amount and writes the updated row; an absent id exits 4; a negative balance is rejected with exit 3. |\n| TXN-004 | Delete a transaction | `txn delete` writes `deleted` and the id; an absent id exits 4; a negative balance is rejected with exit 3. |\n\n### Feature: Transfers\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| XFER-001 | Transfer between accounts | `transfer` writes two `TRANSFER` rows, outgoing first, with the memos stated in the Commands table, or stores neither; the amount is positive and the accounts differ. |\n\n### Feature: Transaction Integrity\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| INTEG-001 | Run each command as one database transaction | Any failing command leaves every table exactly as it was. |\n| INTEG-002 | Enforce the non-negative balance rule | After a command's changes and before commit, any account balance below zero rejects the command with exit 3. |\n\n### Feature: Reports\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| RPT-001 | Report account balances | `report balances` writes one row per account ordered by code, then `TOTAL,,<count>,<sum>`, computed from the database. |\n| RPT-002 | Report category totals | `report categories` writes every category ordered by code, including categories with no transactions, then the `TOTAL` row. |\n| RPT-003 | Report monthly totals | `report monthly` writes one row per `YYYY-MM` with transactions, ascending, then the `TOTAL` row. |\n\n### Feature: Verification\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| VERIFY-001 | Provide the POSIX test runner | `bin/test.sh` runs the pytest suite from the application root against a temporary database and exits zero only when every test passes. |\n| VERIFY-002 | Pass the full supplied grader | `sh sources/run.sh` exits zero with every check passing; this terminal story depends on every implementation story. |\n\n## Surfaced Acceptance Criteria\n\n| ID | Story ID | Criterion |\n|---|---|---|\n| AC-001 | CLI-001 | `ledger --help` and `ledger <command> --help` print usage for the named command to standard output and exit 0; an unknown command still exits 2. |\n| AC-002 | CLI-001 | `ledger` invoked with no arguments writes one usage line to standard error and exits 2. |\n| AC-003 | PERSIST-001 | When the SQLite file named by the target does not exist, the first command creates it empty; a command that then finds no tables exits 1 and writes nothing to standard output. |\n| AC-004 | RPT-001 | On an empty `accounts` table, `report balances` writes the header and `TOTAL,,0,0.00`. |\n| AC-005 | RPT-002 | On empty tables, `report categories` writes the header and `TOTAL,,0,0.00,0.00,0.00`. |\n| AC-006 | RPT-003 | On an empty `transactions` table, `report monthly` writes the header and `TOTAL,0,0.00,0.00,0.00` with no month rows. |\n| AC-007 | TXN-001 | A posted transaction with no memo writes an empty final memo field, with no trailing space. |\n| AC-008 | LOAD-003 | A file whose header lists the transaction columns in a different order, or omits one, is rejected with exit 3 and stores no rows. |\n| AC-009 | INTEG-001 | Two concurrent `ledger` processes against one database each run as one transaction, relying on the database's own transaction isolation with no additional locking. |\n\n## Relationship Model\n\n| Source or group | Relationship type | Related source or group | Evidence | Delivery implication |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md (Commands, Output, Exit codes) | instruction-to-test | sources/run.sh | run.sh checks exit code and stdout of each command against the contract | Command stories must match run.sh byte for byte; Inputs are command arguments, CSV files, and the `DATABASE_URL`, `DATABASE_PATH` settings. Outputs are CSV on standard output for successful commands, one diagnostic line on standard error for failures, and exit codes 0 to 4. The required execution flow is `init`, then `load` of accounts, categories, and transactions, then mutations and reports. Every command runs as one database transaction.\n\n### Story Realization Map\n\n- PERSIST-001: capability; evidence `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md` Database; related shipped persistence library and generated relational layer.\n- PERSIST-002: migration; BEGIN ARTIFACT ANALYSIS.md ===\n# Blueprint Analysis: LayersTesting\n\n## Commander Expectations\n\n- assert the product is an executable `ledger` that keeps cash balances for a small shop in a relational database\n- assert every command either completes in full or changes nothing\n- assert every report is computed from the database and matches the known answers\n- assert the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` exits zero\n- assert no account balance goes negative after any command\n- assert all persistence goes through the shipped persistence library, selected by `DATABASE_URL`\n\n## Crew\n\n| Crew | Charge |\n|---|---|\n| Commander | Defines intent and decides what done means. |\n| Team Lead | Confirms epic completeness and stakeholder expectations. |\n| Planning Crew | Authors atomic specifications and the ordered Manifest. |\n| Shipyard Crew | Builds the tickets without synchronous Commander access. |\n\n## Story List\n\n### Feature: Schema and Persistence\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| PERSIST-001 | Resolve the database target | `DATABASE_URL`, then `DATABASE_PATH`, then `data/app.db`, selects the database; a `postgres://` or `postgresql://` value is a PostgreSQL DSN; other values are SQLite paths with an optional `sqlite://` prefix. |\n| PERSIST-002 | Define the three-table schema | `accounts`, `categories`, and `transactions` exist with the keys and references in the Database section; amounts are integer cents. |\n| PERSIST-003 | Initialize an empty schema | `ledger init` drops the three tables if present, creates them empty, and writes nothing. |\n| PERSIST-004 | Count table rows | `ledger count` writes `table,rows` then the `accounts`, `categories`, and `transactions` rows. |\n\n### Feature: Input Validation\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| VALID-001 | Validate codes, names, descriptions, and memos | Codes are 1 to 12 characters from `A-Z` and `0-9`; names and descriptions are 1 to 40 characters; memos are 0 to 60 characters; none contains a comma or newline; violations exit 3. |\n| VALID-002 | Validate and format amounts | Input matches `-?DIGITS[.D[D]]`, is never zero, and has at most two decimals; output has exactly two decimals and a leading `-` when negative. |\n| VALID-003 | Validate calendar dates | Dates are real calendar dates in `YYYY-MM-DD`; `2026-02-30` exits 3. |\n\n### Feature: Command Line Interface\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| CLI-001 | Dispatch commands and reject usage errors | Unknown command, table, or report, or a wrong argument count, exits 2 with one stderr line; every command has help text. |\n| CLI-002 | Write CSV success output and failure diagnostics | Success writes header and rows as CSV with no quoting, no trailing spaces, and `\\n` endings; `init` writes nothing; failures write nothing to stdout. |\n| CLI-003 | Provide the `ledger` executable at the application root | An executable named `ledger` at the application root runs each command when invoked by the harness from that root. |\n\n### Feature: Bulk Load\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LOAD-001 | Load accounts from a CSV file | A file whose header matches `account,name` loads all rows or none; the seed file loads 4 rows; writes `table,loaded` and `accounts,<rows>`. |\n| LOAD-002 | Load categories from a CSV file | A file whose header matches `category,description` loads all rows or none; the seed file loads 7 rows. |\n| LOAD-003 | Load transactions from a CSV file | A file whose header matches the transaction columns keeps each `txn_id`; any rejected row rejects the file; the seed file loads 120 rows; an unreadable file exits 1. |\n\n### Feature: Accounts\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| ACCT-001 | Add an account | `account add` writes the new account row; a duplicate code or invalid field exits 3. |\n| ACCT-002 | Get an account | `account get` writes the account row with its transaction count and balance; an absent code exits 4. |\n| ACCT-003 | Rename an account | `account rename` writes the updated row; an absent code exits 4; an invalid name exits 3. |\n| ACCT-004 | Delete an account | `account delete` writes `deleted` and the code; an account with transactions is refused with exit 3; an absent code exits 4. |\n\n### Feature: Transactions\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| TXN-001 | Post a transaction | `txn post` writes the new row with id one more than the largest present, or 1 in an empty table; an unknown account or category exits 3. |\n| TXN-002 | Get a transaction | `txn get` writes the row; an absent id exits 4. |\n| TXN-003 | Amend a transaction amount | `txn amend` changes only the amount and writes the updated row; an absent id exits 4; a negative balance is rejected with exit 3. |\n| TXN-004 | Delete a transaction | `txn delete` writes `deleted` and the id; an absent id exits 4; a negative balance is rejected with exit 3. |\n\n### Feature: Transfers\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| XFER-001 | Transfer between accounts | `transfer` writes two `TRANSFER` rows, outgoing first, with the memos stated in the Commands table, or stores neither; the amount is positive and the accounts differ. |\n\n### Feature: Transaction Integrity\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| INTEG-001 | Run each command as one database transaction | Any failing command leaves every table exactly as it was. |\n| INTEG-002 | Enforce the non-negative balance rule | After a command's changes and before commit, any account balance below zero rejects the command with exit 3. |\n\n### Feature: Reports\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| RPT-001 | Report account balances | `report balances` writes one row per account ordered by code, then `TOTAL,,<count>,<sum>`, computed from the database. |\n| RPT-002 | Report category totals | `report categories` writes every category ordered by code, including categories with no transactions, then the `TOTAL` row. |\n| RPT-003 | Report monthly totals | `report monthly` writes one row per `YYYY-MM` with transactions, ascending, then the `TOTAL` row. |\n\n### Feature: Verification\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| VERIFY-001 | Provide the POSIX test runner | `bin/test.sh` runs the pytest suite from the application root against a temporary database and exits zero only when every test passes. |\n| VERIFY-002 | Pass the full supplied grader | `sh sources/run.sh` exits zero with every check passing; this terminal story depends on every implementation story. |\n\n## Surfaced Acceptance Criteria\n\n| ID | Story ID | Criterion |\n|---|---|---|\n| AC-001 | CLI-001 | `ledger --help` and `ledger <command> --help` print usage for the named command to standard output and exit 0; an unknown command still exits 2. |\n| AC-002 | CLI-001 | `ledger` invoked with no arguments writes one usage line to standard error and exits 2. |\n| AC-003 | PERSIST-001 | When the SQLite file named by the target does not exist, the first command creates it empty; a command that then finds no tables exits 1 and writes nothing to standard output. |\n| AC-004 | RPT-001 | On an empty `accounts` table, `report balances` writes the header and `TOTAL,,0,0.00`. |\n| AC-005 | RPT-002 | On empty tables, `report categories` writes the header and `TOTAL,,0,0.00,0.00,0.00`. |\n| AC-006 | RPT-003 | On an empty `transactions` table, `report monthly` writes the header and `TOTAL,0,0.00,0.00,0.00` with no month rows. |\n| AC-007 | TXN-001 | A posted transaction with no memo writes an empty final memo field, with no trailing space. |\n| AC-008 | LOAD-003 | A file whose header lists the transaction columns in a different order, or omits one, is rejected with exit 3 and stores no rows. |\n| AC-009 | INTEG-001 | Two concurrent `ledger` processes against one database each run as one transaction, relying on the database's own transaction isolation with no additional locking. |\n\n## Relationship Model\n\n| Source or group | Relationship type | Related source or group | Evidence | Delivery implication Instructions\n\n### Delivery Shape\n\nThe system is a command-line cash ledger named `ledger`, implemented in Python 3.11 or newer over SQLite through the shipped persistence library. Inputs are command arguments, CSV files, and the `DATABASE_URL`, `DATABASE_PATH` settings. Outputs are CSV on standard output for successful commands, one diagnostic line on standard error for failures, and exit codes 0 to 4. The required execution flow is `init`, then `load` of accounts, categories, and transactions, then mutations and reports. Every command runs as one database transaction.\n\n### Story Realization Map\n\n- PERSIST-001: capability; evidence `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md` Database; related shipped persistence library and generated relational layer.\n- PERSIST-002: migration; evidence `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md` Database table; related generated schema.\n- PERSIST-003: capability; evidence Commands table `init`, `sources/run.sh` sections A and Z.\n- PERSIST-004: capability; evidence Commands table `count`, `sources/run.sh` B4, D3, G6, Z2.\n- VALID-001: capability; evidence Rules 3 and 4.\n- VALID-002: capability; evidence Rule 5.\n- VALID-003: capability; evidence Rule 6.\n- CLI-001: capability; evidence Exit codes table, `sources/run.sh` U1 and U2.\n- CLI-002: capability; evidence Output section, `sources/run.sh` all checks.\n- CLI-003: capability; evidence Objective and Run Harness; related `sources/run.sh`.\n- LOAD-001: capability; evidence Commands `load`, `sources/run.sh` B1, D2; related `sources/seed_accounts.csv`.\n- LOAD-002: capability; evidence Commands `load`, `sources/run.sh` B2; related `sources/seed_categories.csv`.\n- LOAD-003: capability; evidence Commands `load`, Rule 1, `sources/run.sh` B3, D1; related `sources/seed_transactions.csv`, `sources/seed_transactions_bad.csv`.\n- ACCT-001: capability; evidence Commands `account add`, `sources/run.sh` E1, E2.\n- ACCT-002: capability; evidence Commands `account get`, `sources/run.sh` E3, E5, E8.\n- ACCT-003: capability; evidence Commands `account rename`, `sources/run.sh` E4.\n- ACCT-004: capability; evidence Commands `account delete`, `sources/run.sh` E6, E7, E8.\n- TXN-001: capability; evidence Commands `txn post`, Rule 7, `sources/run.sh` F1, F5 to F7, F10.\n- TXN-002: capability; evidence Commands `txn get`, `sources/run.sh` F2, F4.\n- TXN-003: capability; evidence Commands `txn amend`, `sources/run.sh` F3.\n- TXN-004: capability; evidence Commands `txn delete`, `sources/run.sh` F8, F9, G5.\n- XFER-001: capability; evidence Commands `transfer`, `sources/run.sh` G1, G2.\n- INTEG-001: capability; evidence Rule 1, `sources/run.sh` D1, D2, D3.\n- INTEG-002: capability; evidence Commands `report monthly`, `sources/run.sh` C3, H3.\n- VERIFY-001: test harness; evidence Tests section; related pytest suite.\n- VERIFY-002: acceptance contract; evidence Run Harness, `sources/run.sh`; related all seed files.\n\n### Test and Acceptance Strategy\n\nFocused story tests are pytest cases against a real temporary database, each story running only the slice it owns and declaring `Suite: scoped`. The supplied grader has no scope selector, so no story runs it. Exactly one terminal story, VERIFY-002, gates on the complete supplied grader and declares `Suite: full`; it depends on every implementation story. VERIFY-001 runs the application's own pytest suite through `bin/test.sh` and is bounded to the suite the application provides. Sea Trial st-001 is the proof of VERIFY-002, and st-002 is the proof of VERIFY-001.\n\n### Sequencing and Dependencies\n\n- PERSIST-001 and PERSIST-002 precede every other story; CLI-003 precedes every command story.\n- INTEG-001 precedes every mutating story; INTEG-002 precedes TXN-003, TXN-004, XFER-001, and LOAD-003.\n- VALID-001, VALID-002, and VALID-003 precede ACCT, TXN, XFER, and LOAD stories.\n- PERSIST-003 precedes PERSIST-004, LOAD, and all reports.\n- LOAD-001 and LOAD-002 precede LOAD-003 because transactions reference accounts and categories.\n- ACCT-001 precedes TXN-001 because a posted transaction needs an account.\n- Staged fixtures precede VERIFY-002.\n- RPT-001 to RPT-003 follow all loads and mutations they report on.\n- VERIFY-001 follows every implementation story; VERIFY-002 is the terminal story.\n\n### Source Conflicts and Gaps\n\nNo cross-source conflicts remain open. Ambiguities are resolved below by the narrowest behavior consistent with the sources; none is a blocker or questionnaire.\n\n- Database backends: the Stack section names SQLite; the Database section also recognizes a PostgreSQL DSN. Selected behavior: Planning generates the relational layer from the contract stated in the Database section; no library source is required from the Commander.\n- Authentication and authorization: none stated and no protected resource is described.
